# New

A package in scope has no `pipelines.md`. Read `steps.md` (rules) and `template.md` (the file format) here; `examples-new.md` has two worked New rounds. Ends at the Review Gate in `../SKILL.md`.

```
STATUS ─→ WORKFLOWS (ask) ─→ STEPS ─→ LAYOUT ─→ WRITE ─→ [gate: stop]
```

**1. Workflows.** Confirm them with the user as `../SKILL.md` (Asking about workflows) says.

**2. Steps.** Per pipeline, following `steps.md`: an ID from `dt next-id PL` (write the section before the next; never count by hand), Entry, Trigger, Covers, a step table with one owner, `In → Out` in contract types and a real spine version per step, and a pre-build entry sketch of step calls only.

**3. Layout.** One entry module per pipeline, one module (or helper folder) per step owner, each under `build-setup`'s size bar (`steps.md`, Layout).

**4. Write** `docs/packages/<pkg>/pipelines.md` from `template.md`, each pipeline about 35 lines (`dt lint` `budget`). Append the calling PL IDs to each called contract symbol's `Used by:` line (`- **Used by:** forecast; PL-02, PL-03`); that leaves an Approved contract Approved. A step needing a different signature, type or error is a contract change: list it and route to `architecture-design` Revise. Fix every error and `budget` warning `dt lint` reports on every file touched.

# Steps, layout and readability rules

Read before writing or changing a step table or the planned layout. The file format is `template.md`.

## Steps

Per pipeline:
- **Entry** `src/<path>/<entry module>::run_<workflow>`, in the pipeline's own entry module (Layout), **Trigger** (a CLI command or a call from another pipeline), **Covers** (spec IDs, including whole-run `QR-` IDs; a constraint a decision meets, such as a data source's licence under AD-02, goes on the Covers of the step that applies it).
- **Step table**, one row per call in order, plus a row per library class (below): Step (function name; `PL-NN.<function>` is its ID), Owner (one component, or component.module; a step with two owners is two steps), In → Out, Spine version, Covers. A step another pipeline provides says `PL-01 as built`; one following a reference cites it (`as RF-01, per-window fit`).
- **In → Out names contract types.** Every Out, and every In another step's Out feeds, is a contract `type` section (`Demand → Profile` resolves to `### profile.Profile: type`; qualify as `profile.Profile` only when two components define the name), and every CamelCase name in the cell must resolve to one. Entry arguments (dates, regions, a config field) and the last step's plain result (a path, frames) stay lower-case. The build freezes each of these types first, with a sample, so every step's spine and tests start at once against it. A type the contract lacks is a contract change: list it and route to `architecture-design` Revise; never invent it here. `dt lint` checks it (`step-io-types`).
- **Library classes are steps.** A class a factory or lookup step returns, which the pipeline then drives through a Protocol (`make_dataset` returning `SingleCropSet` as a `Dataset`), gets its own row right after its factory's: Step the class name, Owner its module, In → Out its constructor's input → the Protocol, Spine version a minimal real class (200 synthetic leaves, a model guessing at random). The entry sketch never calls it, but the build gives it a spine of its own, so wiring waits on that spine, never on the class's upgrade. Two classes behind one factory are two rows.
- **A provider waiting on a probe** (an external API whose probe isn't Answered or Waived) sits behind a Protocol, and the milestone gets a stand-in: a synthetic class row behind the same factory, in a sibling owner folder (`data.weather_synthetic` → `data/weather_synthetic/`), selected by settings. The real provider keeps its row; `build-planning` plans its spine once the probe is Answered.
- **References** (optional): `**References:** RF-01 (remove_seasonality)` after Covers. Never a `references/` path.
- **Entry sketch** (pre-build): calls to the steps in table order and a return, nothing else. A loop, branch or computation is a missing step.

**Contract `Used by:` lines** get the calling PL IDs after the consumer components; symbol IDs are component-qualified even in a single package (`### profile.fit_seasonality: …`).

**Multi-package:** cross-package *system pipelines* are `architecture-design`'s, in the root. A package-local pipeline that is one segment of one names it: `**Entry:** … · **Trigger:** … · **Part of:** PL-05`. Never edit the root.

## Changing a step table

Revise, and Sync when the doc changes, edit an existing table this way:

- Keep step names where the function survives; a split step gets new rows with new function names. Keep the pipeline's ID: a rebuilt package keeps its PL IDs where it can, so its golden cases still apply.
- If the entry sketch is gone (the spine was built), don't recreate it. The step table is the contract the code must match; the code change becomes a task.
- Keep `Used by:` lines true: add the PL ID to a symbol a new step calls, and remove it from one no step of that pipeline calls any more.

## Layout

One entry module per pipeline, never shared, so two pipelines' wiring never touches one file: `pipeline.py` in the component (single package) or package whose only pipeline it is; where a component runs several, `pipelines/pl<NN>_<workflow>.py` each, and an existing `pipeline.py` stays as it is. One module per step owner (`profile.fit` → `profile/fit.py`; an owner given only as `profile` gets one module per related group of steps). A step whose module needs private helpers owns a folder instead (`core.train` → `core/train/`), holding them; no other step's code goes inside it. A step with its own upgrade beyond the spine gets its own module (`validate/flags.py`, `validate/coverage.py`), so its thicken task never shares a file; only steps built and thickened together share one. In a single package each component gets a subfolder whose `__init__.py` exports its contract only, so a later Grow is a folder move. Modules on no step: only `__init__.py`, the entry modules, `__main__.py` for a `python -m` trigger (dispatching each trigger to its entry), and `types.py`. Test folders sit at the repo root in every layout, multi-package included: `tests/intent/<pkg>/`, `tests/unit/<pkg>/`, never under the package's folder. Plan against `build-setup`'s size bar (500 lines per module, 60 per function, or `CONSTRAINTS.md`'s values): split a module expected to pass it now.

## Readability rules

`build-setup` copies these into `CONSTRAINTS.md`; builders and reviewers enforce them.

- One entry function per pipeline, reading as calls to its named steps; each step owned by one component, its function living there.
- No registries, plugin dispatch or config-driven wiring unless an `AD-NN` says so. A new abstraction needs a second real implementation or a seam the architecture named.
- Every spine version is real and minimal, often the baseline, never a mock, stub or hard-coded return; it stays as what upgrades must beat. Only a step calling another pipeline or package may run against that boundary's fixture-backed fake, until the milestone's system task swaps in the real provider; a step consuming another step's output is built and tested on that type's frozen sample, never on a fake of the step. An object with behavior (a Protocol or class) or a call into another component's function is frozen as its Protocol or signature plus a fixture-backed replay double, which is that type's sample, not a fake.
- A decision-backed exception goes on the row or pipeline, citing it (`dataset lookup by name (AD-06)`).
# pipelines.md template

The file format every mode writes. `dt` parses it: keep the header line, the `## PL-NN: Title` headings, the six step-table columns and the `(pre-build — removed by spine task)` headings exactly. The rules for filling it are in `steps.md`.

````markdown
# <pkg> — Pipelines

**Package:** <pkg> · **Status:** Draft · **Last updated:** YYYY-MM-DD · **Contract:** [contract.md](contract.md)

## PL-NN: <Workflow name>
**Entry:** `src/<path>/<entry module>::run_<workflow>` · **Trigger:** `python -m <module> <command>`
**Covers:** <spec IDs, ranges with an en dash>
**References:** <optional: RF-NN (step) for each reference that informs a step>

| # | Step | Owner | In → Out | Spine version | Covers |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `<function>` | <component[.module]> | <contract types> → <contract type> | <real, minimal baseline> | <spec IDs> |
| 2 | `<Class>` | <component.module> | <constructor input> → <Protocol> | <minimal real class> | <spec IDs> |

### Entry sketch (pre-build — removed by spine task)
```python
def run_<workflow>(cfg: <Config>) -> <Result>:
    a = step_one(cfg.x)
    b = step_two(a)
    return step_three(b, cfg.out)
```

## Planned layout (pre-build — removed by spine task)
```
src/<pkg>/
  __init__.py   contract exports only
  __main__.py   CLI: each trigger → its entry
  pipeline.py   PL-NN entry; with several pipelines here, pipelines/pl<NN>_<workflow>.py each
  <owner modules, one per step owner; a folder for one with private helpers>
tests/intent/<pkg>/  tests/unit/<pkg>/
```

## Open questions
- <question> (assumed: <the default used>)

## Retired
- ~~PL-NN: <title>~~ Retired YYYY-MM-DD: <reason>
````

- Row 2 is a library class its factory in row 1 returns (`steps.md`); drop it when there is none.
- Multi-package segment of a system pipeline: add `· **Part of:** PL-NN` to the pipeline's Entry/Trigger line.
- Multi-package layout: the code block starts at the package's path from the root architecture (`packages/<pkg>/src/<pkg>/`); the test line stays `tests/intent/<pkg>/  tests/unit/<pkg>/`, at the repo root (`steps.md`, Layout).
- After the wiring task, `dt prebuild-clear` replaces each entry sketch with `Layout: \`dt layout <pkg>\`` and drops the planned layout.

# New: worked examples

Two New rounds, each from `dt status` to the gate. The format is `template.md`.

## Example 1: GridLoad, M1 (New, single package)

**Context:** the architecture is Approved, single package `gridload`, components `data`, `profile`, `forecast`. M1 covers DATA-01, DATA-02, PROF-01–PROF-04, QR-01, QR-02. `forecast` is M2.

```
$ dt status --skill pipeline-design
skill: pipeline-design
mode: new
next ids: AD-04 PL-01 TC-01 M3
packages: gridload=new
uncovered_ids: none  (and the other lists: none)
```

In New mode the lists stay empty until the gate's re-run; the scope comes from the architecture's Covers.

**Interview:**

```
Candidates for M1 (nothing is written until you confirm):
1. `load` — pull hourly readings for the meter registry and a date range   M1
2. `season-report` — daily and weekly Fourier fit on loaded demand          M1
3. `holdout` — seasonal-naive forecast scored on held-out weeks             M2 (later)
Q:       Which will you actually run in M1?   (multi-select; recommended: 1, 2)
User:    1 and 2. And the report should be runnable on its own; it can load readings itself.

Q:       Should `season-report` read readings saved by `load`, or call the loader directly?
GUESS:   call it directly; a separate cache step is more than M1 needs.
DEFAULT: call it directly.
User:    Directly.
```

**`contract.md` after the write** (only `Used by:` changes; each step-table type already has its `type` section):

```markdown
### profile.fit_seasonality: Seasonal profile fit
`def fit_seasonality(demand: Demand, *, terms: Terms = "daily+weekly") -> Profile`
- **Invariants:** one sine/cosine pair per harmonic; `residuals` has the input's length and mean zero
- **Errors:** `ValueError` if there are fewer than 168 hours or the input contains NaN
- **Used by:** forecast; PL-02
```

**Result — `docs/packages/gridload/pipelines.md`:**

````markdown
# gridload — Pipelines

**Package:** gridload · **Status:** Draft · **Last updated:** 2026-10-16 · **Contract:** [contract.md](contract.md)

## PL-01: Load readings
**Entry:** `src/gridload/data/pipeline.py::run_load_readings` · **Trigger:** `python -m gridload.data load`
**Covers:** DATA-01, DATA-02, QR-01

| # | Step | Owner | In → Out | Spine version | Covers |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `read_raw` | data.raw | dates → RawReadings | read a synthetic CSV | DATA-01 |
| 2 | `apply_registry` | data.registry | RawReadings, dates → Readings | static registry, no decommissioned meters | DATA-02 |
| 3 | `save_readings` | data.store | Readings → path | one Parquet file per run | DATA-01 |

### Entry sketch (pre-build — removed by spine task)
```python
def run_load_readings(cfg: LoadConfig) -> Path:
    rows = read_raw(cfg.start, cfg.end)
    readings = apply_registry(rows, cfg.start, cfg.end)
    return save_readings(readings, cfg.out)
```

## PL-02: Seasonality report
**Entry:** `src/gridload/profile/pipeline.py::run_season_report` · **Trigger:** `python -m gridload.profile season-report`
**Covers:** PROF-01–PROF-04, QR-02

| # | Step | Owner | In → Out | Spine version | Covers |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `load_readings` | data | regions, dates → Readings | PL-01 as built | DATA-01 |
| 2 | `fit_seasonality` | profile.fit | Demand → Profile | daily and weekly Fourier terms | PROF-02 |
| 3 | `trim_terms` | profile.terms | Profile, n_hours → Profile | keep every term (baseline) | PROF-03 |
| 4 | `residual_spectrum` | profile.spectrum | Profile → Spectrum | full periodogram of the residuals | PROF-01 |
| 5 | `write_report` | profile.report | Spectrum → SeasonalityReport | print table | PROF-04 |

### Entry sketch (pre-build — removed by spine task)
```python
def run_season_report(cfg: SeasonConfig) -> SeasonalityReport:
    readings = load_readings(cfg.regions, cfg.start, cfg.end)
    profile = fit_seasonality(readings.demand())
    profile = trim_terms(profile, n_hours=len(readings))
    spectrum = residual_spectrum(profile)
    return write_report(spectrum, cfg.out)
```

## Planned layout (pre-build — removed by spine task)
```
src/gridload/
  data/
    __init__.py   contract exports only (load_readings, Readings)
    __main__.py   CLI: load
    pipeline.py   PL-01 entry
    raw.py  registry.py  store.py
  profile/
    __init__.py   contract exports only (fit_seasonality, trim_terms)
    __main__.py   CLI: season-report
    pipeline.py   PL-02 entry
    fit.py  terms.py  spectrum.py  report.py
tests/intent/gridload/  tests/unit/gridload/
```

## Open questions
- Should `trim_terms` take the hour count from Profile instead of a parameter? (assumed: parameter)

## Retired

## Example 2: LeafLab, single-crop set (New, single package, another domain)

**Context:** single package `leaflab`, components `core` (Dataset and Model protocols, training loop, evaluation, run storage), `singlecrop`, `models`, `baselines`, `viewer`. AD-03 lets the CLI pick a dataset by name from one dict in `core/datasets.py`. Spec: DS-01–DS-04, MOD-01, TRN-01, EVAL-01, VIEW-01, QR-01 (under an hour on a laptop CPU), QR-02 (same seed, same run).

**Interview:**

```
Candidates for the single-crop set (nothing is written until you confirm):
1. `train singlecrop` — train a small CNN and save the run
2. `gallery <run>` — browse a saved run's predictions
3. `compare <run>` — learning curve against majority-class and logistic baselines
Q:       Which will you actually run?   (multi-select; recommended: 1, 2, 3)
User:    1 and 2. Training should show it beats majority-class and the logistic baseline, inside `train`.
```

**Caught at Step 2:** the first entry sketch looped over epochs; that loop is logic, so it became the step `train` (`core.train`).

**Result — `docs/packages/leaflab/pipelines.md`** (excerpt):

````markdown
## PL-01: Train a model
**Entry:** `src/leaflab/core/pipeline.py::run_train` · **Trigger:** `python -m leaflab train singlecrop --model cnn`
**Covers:** DS-01–DS-04, MOD-01, TRN-01, EVAL-01, QR-01, QR-02

| # | Step | Owner | In → Out | Spine version | Covers |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `make_dataset` | core.datasets | dataset name, seed → Dataset | lookup by name (AD-03) | DS-01 |
| 2 | `SingleCropSet` | singlecrop.dataset | seed → Dataset | 200 synthetic leaves, fixed split | DS-02–DS-04 |
| 3 | `make_model` | models.factory | DatasetSpec, ModelConfig → Model | always the CNN | MOD-01 |
| 4 | `SmallCnn` | models.cnn | DatasetSpec → Model | uniform random guess | MOD-01 |
| 5 | `train` | core.train | Dataset, Model, epochs → Model, Log | epoch loop calling fit_batch; CSV log | TRN-01 |
| 6 | `run_baselines` | baselines | Dataset, seeds → Scores | majority class only | EVAL-01 |
| 7 | `evaluate` | core.evaluate | Model, Dataset, Scores → Curve | held-out accuracy over 5 seeds | EVAL-01 |
| 8 | `save_run` | core.runs | Model, Log, Curve → RunDir | folder with config.json, weights.pt, log.csv | QR-02 |

### Entry sketch (pre-build — removed by spine task)
```python
def run_train(cfg: TrainConfig) -> RunDir:
    dataset = make_dataset(cfg.dataset, seed=cfg.seed)
    model = make_model(dataset.spec, cfg.model)
    model, log = train(dataset, model, epochs=cfg.epochs)
    scores = run_baselines(dataset, seeds=cfg.eval_seeds)
    curve = evaluate(model, dataset, scores)
    return save_run(model, log, curve, cfg.out)
```

## PL-02: Browse predictions
**Entry:** `src/leaflab/viewer/pipeline.py::run_gallery` · **Trigger:** `python -m leaflab gallery runs/<id>`
**Covers:** VIEW-01

| # | Step | Owner | In → Out | Spine version | Covers |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `load_run` | core.runs | RunDir → Run | read the run folder | VIEW-01 |
| 2 | `predict` | core.predict | Run, seed → Predictions | predict the held-out split | QR-02 |
| 3 | `render` | viewer.render | Predictions → pages | text table of label vs prediction | VIEW-01 |

## Planned layout (pre-build — removed by spine task)
```
src/leaflab/
  __main__.py     CLI: train, gallery
  core/
    __init__.py   contract exports only (Dataset, Model, Run)
    pipeline.py   PL-01 entry
    datasets.py   name → dataset constructor (AD-03)
    train/        core.train's folder: its private helpers (batches.py, checkpoint.py)
    evaluate.py  runs.py  predict.py
  singlecrop/ __init__.py  dataset.py
  models/     __init__.py  factory.py  cnn.py
  baselines/  __init__.py  majority.py
  viewer/     __init__.py  pipeline.py (PL-02 entry)  render.py
tests/intent/leaflab/  tests/unit/leaflab/
```
````

Notes:
- `SingleCropSet` and `SmallCnn` are rows of their own: the factories return them and `train` drives them through `Dataset` and `Model`, so each gets its own spine, and wiring waits on those spines, never on the real CNN.
- The random-guess model and the text table are real spines: the floor the CNN and the image gallery must beat.
- Each pipeline has its own entry module (`core/pipeline.py`, `viewer/pipeline.py`), so their wiring tasks never share a file; `__main__.py` dispatches both triggers.
- The dataset lookup is a registry, allowed only because AD-03 names it on the row.
- The logistic baseline is a later step upgrade of `run_baselines` (a task), not a new step.

# Quant Research Platform — Architecture

**Status:** Approved · **Shape:** Multi-package · **Last updated:** 2026-10-17 · **Spec:** [spec.md](spec.md)

## Overview
`data` downloads Sharadar, FRED and SEC data into an immutable, bitemporal snapshot store, validates each snapshot and promotes it, and serves every later stage one point-in-time view. `engine` is the shared method every stage is scored with: the walk-forward schedule and look-ahead guard, the hypothesis ledger, the backtest and the portfolio constraints. `lab` holds the S2–S3 research (factors, denoising, covariance ranking, classical portfolios, features, significance) and the final verdict. `models` holds the torch-based S4 work: forecasters, forecast-driven portfolios and PPO agents, trained on the laptop or on a rented GPU. `server` is a local-only HTTP API over `data` that the TypeScript `app` reads for the explorer and the live run monitor.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
  vendors[(Sharadar · FRED · SEC EDGAR)] --> data
  data --> engine
  engine --> lab
  data --> lab
  engine --> models
  lab --> models
  data --> models
  data --> server
  server -- HTTP + SSE, wire schemas --> app
  models -. training bundle .-> gpu[(rented GPU)]
```

## Packages
| Package | Path | Responsibility | Covers | Depends on | Doc |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `data` | packages/data | Download, store, validate and serve point-in-time daily data and run records | UNIV-01–UNIV-04, INGST-01–INGST-06, VALID-01–VALID-08, PANEL-01–PANEL-06, DATA-01–DATA-07, QR-01, QR-03 | — | [data](packages/data/architecture.md) |
| `server` | packages/server | Local HTTP + SSE API over `data`; owns the wire schemas | EXPL-01–EXPL-05 (backend), RUNS-01–RUNS-04 (backend), QR-04, QR-06, QR-08 | `data` | [server](packages/server/architecture.md) |
| `app` | packages/app | Browser explorer and live run monitor | EXPL-01–EXPL-06, RUNS-01–RUNS-04, QR-06, QR-08 | `server` (HTTP only) | [app](packages/app/architecture.md) |

32	## Dependency rules
33	- `data` imports nothing internal. Only `data` writes the snapshot store or run records; everything else reads through its contract.
34	- `engine` may import `data` only.
35	- `lab` may import `engine` and `data`; never `models`, `server`.
36	- `models` may import `lab`, `engine` and `data`; it is the only package that imports `torch`, `gymnasium` or `stable_baselines3`.
37	- `server` may import `data` only, and only `server` imports `fastapi`.
38	- `app` imports no Python; it talks to `server` over HTTP through the generated wire types.
39	- Enforced by `import-linter` contracts over the uv workspace and a forbidden-import check for the heavy stacks (AD-01).
40	
41	## Cross-package contracts
42	- `data.membership_as_of`, `data.returns_panel`, `data.bars_panel`, `data.risk_free`, `data.sectors`, `data.Calendar` — provided by `data`, used by `engine`, `lab`, `models`, `server`. [Contract](packages/data/contract.md)
43	- `data.current_snapshot`, `data.list_snapshots`, `data.stock_history`, `data.find_stock`, `data.coverage`, `data.issues`, `data.list_runs`, `data.run_events` — provided by `data`, used by `server`. [Contract](packages/data/contract.md)
44	- `server` wire types and routes (`server.MembershipBody` … `server.stream_run`) — provided by `server`, used by `app`. [Contract](packages/server/contract.md)
45	- `engine.Schedule`, `engine.training_window`, `engine.guard`, ledger (`engine.register` … `engine.score_lockbox`), `engine.backtest`, `engine.check_weights`, `engine.project_capped` — provided by `engine`, used by `lab`, `models`. [Contract](packages/engine/contract.md)
46	- `lab.forward_targets`, `lab.compute_features`, `lab.surviving_features`, `lab.min_variance`, `lab.benchmark` — provided by `lab`, used by `models`. [Contract](packages/lab/contract.md)
47	- `models` publishes no symbol another package imports; its results reach `lab.write_verdict` only through ledger entries. [Contract](packages/models/contract.md)

98	## Project layout
99	```
100	packages/data/     → qrp_data (Python)
101	packages/engine/   → qrp_engine (Python)
102	packages/lab/      → qrp_lab (Python)
103	packages/models/   → qrp_models (Python, torch)
104	packages/server/   → qrp_server (Python, FastAPI)
105	packages/app/      → TypeScript + React (Vite)
106	store/             → snapshot store, run and ledger databases (git-ignored, AD-06)
107	results/           → per-ledger-entry outputs and reports (git-ignored)
108	```
109	
110	## Shared tech stack and tooling
111	| Area | Choice | Decision |
112	|---|---|---|
113	| Workspace | uv workspace, one lockfile; npm for `packages/app` | AD-01 |
114	| Language | Python 3.12; TypeScript 5.6 | AD-02 |
115	| Numerics | numpy 2.1, pandas 2.2, scipy 1.14, pandera 0.20 | AD-02 |
116	| Prices, actions, membership, identity | Sharadar SEP, ACTIONS, TICKERS, SP500 via `nasdaq-data-link` 1.0 | AD-03 |
117	| Risk-free rate | FRED DTB3 | AD-04 |
118	| Sector history | SEC EDGAR SIC codes, or Sharadar if PB-01 shows history | AD-05 |
119	| Trading calendar | `exchange_calendars` 4.13 (XNYS), cross-checked with SEP dates | AD-08 |
120	| Store | Parquet (pyarrow 17) + DuckDB 1.1; SQLite (WAL) for runs and ledger | AD-06, AD-07, AD-11 |
121	| Local API | FastAPI 0.115, uvicorn 0.32, pydantic 2.9, sse-starlette 2.1 | AD-09 |
122	| App | React 18.3, Vite 5.4, TanStack Query 5, React Router 6, Observable Plot 0.6 | AD-09 |
123	| Wire types | pydantic → JSON Schema → `json-schema-to-typescript` 15; ajv 8 in app tests | AD-09 |
124	| Optimisation | cvxpy 1.5 (Clarabel) | AD-14 |
125	| Neural nets | PyTorch 2.4 | AD-16 |
126	| RL | gymnasium 0.29, stable-baselines3 2.3 | AD-17 |
127	| Lint, types, tests | ruff, mypy, pytest, hypothesis; eslint, tsc, vitest; import-linter | AD-01 |
128	
129	Packages are versioned together; one release tag for the repo.
130	
131	## Cross-cutting concerns
132	- **Reproducibility (QR-07, PANEL-05):** every computation takes an explicit `SnapshotId`; every ledger entry records snapshot, config hash, seed and git SHA; CPU code is deterministic given those (no unseeded randomness, stable sorts).
133	- **Point-in-time discipline (PANEL-01, WFWD-04):** `data` filters by known-date; `engine.guard` checks every fit input against its `engine.Window` and raises `engine.LookAheadError`.
134	- **Claims (LEDG-05):** every comparison, ranking or claim is produced inside a started ledger entry and written under `results/<entry>/`; nothing else reports results (AD-11).
135	- **Skills:** `data-analysis` runs the quality report (VALID-07) and the characterisation steps (FACT-03, COV-02, FEAT-04); `null-hypothesis-testing` runs every ledger test (AD-11); `dataviz` and `frontend-design` govern the app (EXPL-06). Each component that runs a step names it.
136	- **Errors:** typed exceptions per contract; runs record failures per stock and carry on (INGST edge cases); CLI exits non-zero on a blocking failure.
137	- **Data costs (QR-01):** paid sources: Sharadar (existing subscription, $0 extra). FRED, SEC EDGAR, `exchange_calendars`: free. Extra monthly cost: $0.
138	- **Data locality (QR-04, QR-05):** the server binds 127.0.0.1 and checks the `Host` header; the only outbound vendor-data transfer is the GPU training bundle (AD-18).
139	- **Performance (QR-03, QR-08):** update downloads fetch by `lastupdated` (PB-01); promotion builds per-snapshot read views sorted by stock; run timings are stored in run records.
140	- **Configuration:** one `qrp.toml` (store path, API keys by env var name, server port); research settings live in each ledger registration.
141	
142	## Decisions
143	### AD-01: Multi-package uv workspace with a separate TypeScript app
144	**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** system
145	- **Context:** The brief fixes Python for analysis and TypeScript/React for the app (two runtimes, QR-06). S4 brings torch, gymnasium and stable-baselines3 and a rented GPU (QR-02, QR-05), a stack the data and S2–S3 code should not carry. S1–S3 together would exceed ~20 modules, past the ~12-module grow trigger, and the shared method (schedule, ledger, backtest) is used by S2–S4 alike.
146	- **Decision:** Six packages: `data`, `engine`, `lab`, `models`, `server` (Python, one uv workspace and lockfile) and `app` (npm). Dependency rules above, enforced by import-linter and a forbidden-import check in CI and the pre-commit hook.
147	- **Alternatives:** One Python package plus the app — mixes torch into S1–S3 installs and blurs the "only `data` writes the store" rule. Separate repos — version skew between the server's wire schemas and the app, for one person.
148	- **Consequences:** A component that grows out of its package moves by package, not by repo. Each package has its own architecture and contract docs. The GPU host installs `models` with its dependencies but receives only the training bundle (AD-18).
149	
150	### AD-02: Python 3.12 numerics stack and TypeScript 5.6
151	**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** system
152	- **Context:** The brief requires Python for analysis and TypeScript/React for the app. Panels are dates × ~1,200 stocks over ~28 years: small enough for in-memory pandas (QR-03).
153	- **Decision:** Python 3.12 with numpy 2, pandas 2.2 (dates × `StockId` frames, NaN for missing per PANEL-04), scipy, pandera schemas for every frame that crosses a step; TypeScript 5.6 strict.
154	- **Alternatives:** polars — faster, but the reference code (RF-01–RF-03) and cvxpy/scikit-style libraries are pandas/numpy-shaped. xarray — adds a layer with no requirement needing it.
155	- **Consequences:** Frames carry NaN for missing values everywhere; no function may `fillna(0)` on returns (enforced by a lint rule and contract invariants).
156	
157	### AD-03: Sharadar for prices, corporate actions, membership and identity
158	**Status:** Proposed · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** `data`
159	- **Context:** DATA-01–DATA-04 and UNIV-01–UNIV-03 need delisted stocks, membership with effective dates for ≥15 years, splits, dividends, spin-offs, ticker changes and delistings, and a permanent identifier; QR-01 caps extra spend at $50/month and the brief has a Sharadar subscription. Sharadar's [prices page](https://sharadar.com/prices) (read 2026-10-17) states SEP covers active and delisted US stocks with history to December 1997, an `sp500` table with "additions and deletions with effective dates" from January 1998, an `actions` table of splits, dividends, spin-offs, ticker changes and delisting details, `close` split-adjusted, `closeadj` adjusted for splits, dividends and spin-offs, and daily updates. The [fundamentals page](https://sharadar.com/fundamentals) (read 2026-10-17) states TICKERS has a permanent `permaticker` key and ACTIONS covers listing and delisting dates, delist reasons and acquisition counterparties. Neither page says whether delisting payouts carry a value, whether TICKERS keeps sector history, or how revisions show in `lastupdated`.
160	- **Decision:** Sharadar SEP (OHLCV, `closeunadj`), ACTIONS, TICKERS and SP500 through the Nasdaq Data Link tables API, behind the `data` `Source` protocol. `StockId` is the `permaticker`; each share class has its own (UNIV-03). Full downloads use the bulk export; updates filter on `lastupdated` (INGST-02).
161	- **Alternatives:** CRSP via WRDS — the reference standard for delisting returns, but needs an institutional licence. Norgate Data — has historical S&P membership and delisted stocks, but a second paid subscription close to the cap and Windows-centred tooling. Free scraped constituents (Wikipedia, EODHD free tier) — accurate only back to about 2000 per those sources, and no delisted prices.
162	- **Consequences:** History starts 1998, so DATA-01's 15 years holds with ~13 to spare. The adapter is the only Sharadar-aware code; a switch touches `data.vendors` only. Delisting returns depend on what ACTIONS records (AD-08).
163	- **Probe:** PB-01 — on the user's subscription: do SEP/ACTIONS/SP500/TICKERS return delisted members back to 1998; do ACTIONS delisting and acquisition rows carry a payout value; does TICKERS keep sector history; do `lastupdated` filters return revisions; and how long does a bulk export take?
164	
165	### AD-04: FRED DTB3 for the daily risk-free rate
166	**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** `data`
167	- **Context:** PANEL-06 and DATA-06 need a daily short-term rate on every trading date; QR-01 limits cost. The [FRED DTB3 page](https://fred.stlouisfed.org/series/DTB3) (read 2026-10-17) describes the 3-Month Treasury Bill secondary market rate, discount basis, daily, from the Federal Reserve's H.15 release.
168	- **Decision:** Download DTB3 via the FRED CSV endpoint; convert the discount rate to a bond-equivalent yield and then to a daily simple rate over the next trading day; on an exchange trading day with no H.15 value (bond-market holidays) carry the prior value with `filled=True`.
169	- **Alternatives:** Kenneth French daily `RF` — derived from a monthly rate and published with a lag. Effective federal funds rate — an overnight bank rate rather than a T-bill investable rate.
170	- **Consequences:** Free (QR-01). Carried values are visible in the panel and the quality report.
171	- **Probe:** none — the FRED series page documents the series, frequency and source; history depth is checked by VALID-06's coverage check on every snapshot.
172	
173	### AD-05: Point-in-time sectors from SEC EDGAR SIC codes
174	**Status:** Proposed · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** `data`
175	- **Context:** DATA-07 and FCST-03 need each stock's sector on each date. Sharadar's [fundamentals page](https://sharadar.com/fundamentals) (read 2026-10-17) lists sector, industry, SIC sector, SIC industry and Fama industry fields on TICKERS but says nothing about history, so it may be the current value only. The SEC's company filing metadata records an SIC code with each filer, and is free (QR-01).
176	- **Decision:** If PB-01 shows TICKERS keeps dated classifications, use them. Otherwise build sector intervals from the SIC code recorded on each company's EDGAR filings, mapped to SIC divisions, linked to `StockId` by the CIK on TICKERS; a stock's first known sector is backfilled to its first membership date and flagged `assumed`.
177	- **Alternatives:** GICS history from S&P — licensed, beyond the budget. Current Sharadar sector for all dates — fails DATA-07's reclassification check.
178	- **Consequences:** SIC divisions are coarser than GICS sectors (10 vs 11, different boundaries); only FCST-03 uses sectors, as a fixed attribute.
179	- **Probe:** PB-02 — does EDGAR expose a per-filing SIC history for delisted S&P 500 members since 1998, and can every member be linked by CIK?
180	
181	### AD-06: Bitemporal Parquet store with immutable snapshot manifests
182	**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** `data`
183	- **Context:** INGST-03–INGST-05, DATA-04, PANEL-01 and PANEL-05 need immutable snapshots, both values of a revision with the date each was known, safe resume, and reproducible pinned reads; QR-03 and QR-08 need fast reads on a laptop.
184	- **Decision:** Every record row carries `ref_date`, `known_date`, `known_basis` (`observed` when first seen by a run; `assumed_ref_date` for backfilled history, known at the close of its reference date) and `run_id`. Runs append new Parquet part files and never modify one. A snapshot is a manifest (`store/snapshots/<id>/manifest.json`) listing the part files it includes plus vendor watermarks; `store/CURRENT` names the promoted snapshot and is replaced by atomic rename. Promotion builds a derived, rebuildable read view per snapshot (latest version per key, sorted by stock and date). DuckDB queries the parts and views.
185	- **Alternatives:** A database (Postgres, DuckDB file) with update-in-place — revisions and immutability need hand-built versioning, and a failed run can corrupt the current state. Full copy per snapshot — simple, but ~1–2 GB per daily update.
186	- **Consequences:** Snapshots share part files, so daily updates add MBs. An as-of-T read takes, per key, the newest version with `known_date ≤ T`. Garbage collection of unreferenced parts is a later manual command.
187	
188	### AD-07: Run records and live events in SQLite, pushed by the server over SSE
189	**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** `data`, `server`
190	- **Context:** INGST-06 and RUNS-01–RUNS-04 need durable run records and ≤2 s live updates that survive the app being closed; the "process dies" edge case needs detection within 10 s. Runs start from the CLI, not the app.
191	- **Decision:** Run processes write the run row and an append-only event table (`seq`, time, kind, stock, counts, message) to `store/runs.sqlite` in WAL mode, plus a heartbeat every 2 s. `data.run_record` reports a `running` run with no heartbeat for 10 s as `stopped_unexpectedly`. The server polls events by `seq` every 0.5 s and streams them over SSE; a new subscriber first gets the run's state so far (RUNS-04).
192	- **Alternatives:** WebSocket from the run process to the server — couples the run to the server being up. Log-file tailing — no structured counts, harder to resume by `seq`.
193	- **Consequences:** No message broker; the run process never needs the server. Worst-case latency ≈ poll interval + SSE flush, well under 2 s.
194	
195	### AD-08: Returns built from unadjusted prices, actions and the exchange calendar
196	**Status:** Proposed · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** `data`
197	- **Context:** VALID-05 needs adjusted returns equal to economic total return within 0.01 pp; PANEL-03 needs delisting returns (cash payout, or −100% flagged estimated); PANEL-04 forbids filled gaps; DATA-05 and VALID-03 need the exchange calendar. RF-03 counts horizons in rows, which spec FCST-01 forbids.
198	- **Decision:** Daily total return = (unadjusted close × split factor + cash dividend) / previous unadjusted close − 1, with spin-offs taken from Sharadar's `closeadj` ratio on the ex-date; VALID-05 cross-checks every action day against `closeadj`. Delisting return: the recorded payout or final price versus the last close; with neither, −100% and `estimated=True`. Trading days come from `exchange_calendars` XNYS; a date where SEP has bars for most members but XNYS says closed (or the reverse) fails validation.
199	- **Alternatives:** Use `closeadj` returns directly — no independent check of VALID-05 and no delisting payouts. A hand-maintained holiday list — error-prone for special closures.
200	- **Consequences:** Every return in the platform comes from `data.returns_panel`; horizons everywhere count XNYS sessions (AD-15).
201	- **Probe:** PB-01 — whether ACTIONS gives payouts determines how many delisting returns are `estimated`.
202	
203	### AD-09: Local FastAPI server with wire schemas generated into the app
204	**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** `server`, `app`
205	- **Context:** QR-04 (local-only), QR-06 (each shape declared once and checked on both sides), QR-08 (≤2 s stock page, ≤1 s membership) and RUNS-01 (≤2 s live updates).
206	- **Decision:** `server` is FastAPI on uvicorn bound to `127.0.0.1`, rejecting any non-loopback `Host` header. Every response body is a pydantic model in `qrp_server.wire`; `qrp server export-schema` writes JSON Schema to `packages/app/src/wire/schema.json`, and `json-schema-to-typescript` generates `wire.ts`. CI fails when the regenerated schema or `wire.ts` differs from the committed one; server tests validate responses against the models, and app tests validate fixtures with ajv. Live runs use SSE.
207	- **Alternatives:** Hand-written TS types — drift is invisible (fails QR-06). OpenAPI client generators — generate whole clients we don't need; the schema-only path is smaller.
208	- **Consequences:** Python is the single source of every wire shape; changing one field on one side fails the check (QR-06's test).
209	
210	### AD-10: One walk-forward schedule with per-method gaps and a look-ahead guard
211	**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** `engine`
212	- **Context:** WFWD-01–WFWD-06 fix 21-day test blocks after ≥756 days, a gap ≥ max(5, longest label horizon), fit-time look-ahead errors and a 504-day lockbox. FCST-02, FACT-01, DENOI-03 and AGENT-01 rely on the same windows.
213	- **Decision:** `engine.schedule(snapshot)` derives the test blocks once from the snapshot's calendar: the lockbox is the last 504 sessions, and test blocks tile the sessions from 757 to the lockbox start. A method gets its training window from `engine.training_window(period, horizon=…, length=…)`, which sets the gap to max(5, horizon) and ends training before it; length defaults to 756 sessions (rolling), and a registration may choose expanding. Every fit entry point passes its inputs through `engine.guard`, which raises `engine.LookAheadError` on any row dated after the window's cutoff, or any label whose horizon ends on or after the test start.
214	- **Alternatives:** A schedule per method — breaks WFWD-01. Trusting callers to slice correctly — WFWD-04 needs a failing run, not a convention.
215	- **Consequences:** Test periods are identical across methods by construction; only the training cutoffs differ by horizon. A period a method cannot fit is scored as a failure (WFWD edge case), never dropped.
216	
217	### AD-11: SQLite hypothesis ledger, with tests run through the null-hypothesis-testing skill
218	**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** `engine`
219	- **Context:** LEDG-01–LEDG-06 need frozen registrations, outcomes for failures too, families for correction, and no unregistered claims; WFWD-05 needs a once-only lockbox gate; QR-07 needs reproducible entries.
220	- **Decision:** `store/ledger.sqlite` holds registrations (with a content hash), runs, outcomes and lockbox scores; triggers forbid updating a registration once its run has started. Every comparison names its correction family at registration; the family's members (abandoned and incomplete ones included) are fixed by the registrations naming it. The test step of every entry is run with the `null-hypothesis-testing` skill: `engine.TestInput` carries the registered null, metric, per-period paired series, family and α; the skill chooses the null distribution and correction; the result is recorded as `engine.TestOutcome`. Results are written under `results/<entry>/` and are readable only through a started entry.
221	- **Alternatives:** Ledger as YAML files in git — easy to edit after the fact (fails LEDG-02). A pre-registration service (OSF) — external and manual for hundreds of entries.
222	- **Consequences:** Every package that makes a claim registers through `engine`'s ledger functions; the families are listed where each stage's component registers them.
223	
224	### AD-12: Factors by PCA with a parallel-analysis count and per-date residual regression
225	**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** `lab`
226	- **Context:** FACT-01–FACT-05: window-only fits, a noise-based factor count (0 kept in ≥95% of noise trials), ≥50% drop in mean absolute correlation, no zero-filling, and residuals as of any date. RF-01 shows the market-mode removal but fits on the full sample, zero-fills gaps and fixes k.
227	- **Decision:** Per training window, keep stocks with ≥60 observations, standardise with window statistics, estimate the pairwise-complete correlation matrix and project it to the nearest PSD matrix. Choose k by parallel analysis: eigenvalue i is kept if it exceeds the 95th percentile of the i-th eigenvalue over 100 column-permuted copies that keep each stock's missing pattern. Factor returns per date come from a cross-sectional regression of that date's available returns on the loadings; residuals are defined only where a return exists. `lab.residual_returns(T)` uses loadings from the window that ends before T.
228	- **Alternatives:** Marchenko–Pastur edge with σ² = 1 — miscalibrated with missing data and fat tails. Fixed k = 1 as in RF-01 — fails FACT-02.
229	- **Consequences:** FACT-02's 95% property is the parallel-analysis threshold by construction, and is checked on simulated noise in tests. The FACT-03 check runs with the `data-analysis` skill.
230	
231	### AD-13: Denoising by constant residual eigenvalue at the fitted Marchenko–Pastur edge
232	**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** `lab`
233	- **Context:** DENOI-01–DENOI-03: keep signal eigenvalues, replace noise eigenvalues by their average, keep the trace, return a valid PD correlation matrix, fit on the window only, including when T < N.
234	- **Decision:** Fit the Marchenko–Pastur σ² to the window's eigenvalue density by maximum likelihood (excluding eigenvalues above the parallel-analysis count of AD-12); eigenvalues below the fitted edge λ₊ = σ²(1+√(N/T))² are replaced by their mean. Rebuild, then rescale to a unit diagonal. DENOI-01 is checked on the eigenvalues before the rescale; DENOI-02 after.
235	- **Alternatives:** Targeted shrinkage of noise eigenvalues only (keeps more structure, but not what DENOI-01 specifies). Clipping at the edge without averaging — not trace-preserving.
236	- **Consequences:** The matrix stays PD whenever the noise eigenvalues' mean is positive, which holds while the trace is N; T/N is recorded per window (DENOI edge case).
237	
238	### AD-14: Portfolio construction with cvxpy, ERC by Newton, HRP in-house
239	**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** `lab`, `engine`
240	- **Context:** PORT-01 (long-only, fully invested, ≤5%), PORT-06 (equal risk contributions within 1%), the infeasible-cap edge case, and COV-01's minimum-variance portfolio; FPORT-03 and AGENT-05 need the same constraints.
241	- **Decision:** Minimum variance: a QP in cvxpy with the Clarabel solver. Risk parity: Spinu's convex formulation solved by Newton's method, then checked against PORT-01 (with ~500 stocks the ERC weights sit far below 5%); if the cap would bind, ERC within 1% (PORT-06) and the cap cannot both hold, so that period is scored as a failure for risk parity, never clipped. HRP: López de Prado's single-linkage recursive bisection, then capped by iterative redistribution. `engine.project_capped` is a Euclidean projection onto the capped simplex for weights from `models`.
242	- **Alternatives:** riskfolio-lib or skfolio for all four — broad, but heavier dependencies and less direct control over the infeasibility errors the spec names. scipy SLSQP for minimum variance — slow and fragile at N≈500.
243	- **Consequences:** One `engine.check_weights` enforces PORT-01 for every method, FPORT and AGENT included.
244	
245	### AD-15: Targets counted in exchange sessions
246	**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** `lab`, `models`
247	- **Context:** FCST-01 needs horizons in trading days with a missing target when any day lacks a bar; SIG-01 needs the T+1..T+5 residual return; WFWD-03 and FCST-02 need each label's end date. RF-03 counts rows, silently drops each stock's last days and does not check prices.
248	- **Decision:** `lab.forward_targets` computes forward log returns, forward realised volatility and forward residual returns over horizons counted on the XNYS calendar; a target is NaN if any session in the horizon lacks a return; each target row carries its `label_end` date so `engine.guard` can enforce the gap. Delisting returns count as the final day's return.
249	- **Alternatives:** Adopt RF-03 as is — fails FCST-01 on halts.
250	- **Consequences:** S3 and S4 share one target definition; features never see `label_end` columns.
251	
252	### AD-16: Plain PyTorch sequence forecasters
253	**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** `models`
254	- **Context:** FCST-01–FCST-07: separate static, known-ahead and observed inputs; point-in-time static attributes with an "unknown" category; training-window normalisation; purged splits; reproducibility within 1e-6. RF-02's window builder has no purge, a full-sample vocabulary and non-PIT statics.
255	- **Decision:** Two models in plain PyTorch 2.4: a compact temporal fusion transformer (variable selection, LSTM encoder, attention) and an LSTM. A window builder derived from RF-02 with its caveats fixed: origin-date splits purged by `engine.guard`, vocabulary and normalisation fit per training window with an `unknown` index, statics read point-in-time from `data.sectors`. Deterministic algorithms on, seeds recorded, and a CPU smoke configuration.
256	- **Alternatives:** pytorch-forecasting — has a TFT, but its own normalisers, encoders and splits fight the walk-forward and the guard. Darts — same issue, plus more dependencies.
257	- **Consequences:** We own the model code (~a few hundred lines). Baselines (trailing 63-day vol, EWMA vol, zero return) run in the same harness.
258	
259	### AD-17: PPO agents with stable-baselines3 in a training-window market
260	**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** `models`
261	- **Context:** AGENT-01–AGENT-05: train only on training-window data, pay BTEST's costs, a 20-configuration budget per line, a stop rule and valid portfolio actions.
262	- **Decision:** A gymnasium environment replays training-window returns through `engine.backtest`'s cost model; actions are logits mapped through softmax and `engine.project_capped` onto the capped simplex. PPO from stable-baselines3. Each line's budget is a ledger registration; the 21st configuration and any run on a stopped line are refused by the ledger.
263	- **Alternatives:** Hand-written PPO — more code to get right for no requirement. RLlib — distributed machinery a laptop/single GPU doesn't need.
264	- **Consequences:** Divergent runs are recorded as failed and count against the budget (AGENT edge case).
265	
266	### AD-18: GPU training bundles, shipped and wiped
267	**Status:** Accepted · **Date:** 2026-10-17 · **Scope:** `models`
268	- **Context:** QR-02 caps GPU spend at $100/month; QR-05 allows training copies on rented GPUs only if deleted at the end of the rental; the brief rents occasionally.
269	- **Decision:** `models` writes a training bundle (windowed, normalised arrays, config, seeds; no raw vendor tables) that is copied over SSH to any hourly GPU host, trained there with the same `models` code, and copied back as checkpoints and forecasts. A teardown command deletes the bundle and checkpoints on the host, verifies the deletion, and logs it with the rental's hours and cost to `store/gpu_log.sqlite`; `qrp models gpu-budget` refuses a new rental when the month's logged spend would pass $100.
270	- **Alternatives:** Cloud notebooks (Colab) — data persists in the user's drive, harder to prove deletion. A fixed cloud provider SDK — ties the design to one vendor for an occasional need.
271	- **Consequences:** The GPU vendor stays an open choice; any SSH-reachable host works. Spend tracking is from logged hours × rate, reconciled with invoices by hand (QR-02's check).
272	
273	## Risks and open questions
274	- PB-01 (Sharadar coverage on the user's subscription) gates AD-03, AD-05 and AD-08; if delisting payouts are missing, many delisting returns are `estimated` −100%, biasing results downward. Hand-off to `api-probe`.
275	- PB-02 (EDGAR SIC history) gates AD-05; if neither source has history, DATA-07 needs a spec change. Hand-off to `api-probe`.
276	- UNIV-01's 495–510 count: Sharadar quarterly snapshots plus change rows may disagree around same-day add/remove pairs; VALID-06 surfaces it, and the UNIV-04 check set (≥30 announced changes, ≥5 delisted) is collected by the researcher from S&P press releases before UNIV-04's test is built.
277	- QR-03's 6-hour full rebuild depends on the bulk export speed (PB-01).
278	- GPU rental vendor is not chosen; any SSH host fits AD-18.

284	# Quant Research Platform — Roadmap
285	
286	**Last updated:** 2026-10-17
287	
288	| Stage | Name | Source | Status* |
289	|---|---|---|---|
290	| S1 | Trusted data & explorer | brief | Specced |
291	| S2 | Risk structure & classical portfolios | brief | Specced |
292	| S3 | Features & significance | brief | Specced |
293	| S4 | Forecasts & agents | brief | Specced |
294	
295	*Written by `dt roadmap` from doc statuses and todo checkboxes; never hand-edited.
296	
297	## S1: Trusted data & explorer
298	**Outline:** Download, clean, validate and store daily S&P 500 data with membership on each date, delisted stocks and corporate actions, explore it, and watch download and validation runs live in a local browser app.
299	**Skills:** api-probe, data-analysis, dataviz, frontend-design
300	
301	## S2: Risk structure & classical portfolios
302	**Outline:** Remove the market and other dominant factors, denoise the correlation matrix, and rank covariance estimators and minimum-variance, risk-parity and hierarchical risk-parity portfolios on realised out-of-sample variance, using the shared walk-forward engine with every comparison recorded in the hypothesis ledger.
303	**Skills:** data-analysis, null-hypothesis-testing
304	
305	## S3: Features & significance
306	**Outline:** Build candidate features and test each for information gain over market-removed returns, with every claim tested against chance and its outcome recorded in the ledger.
307	**Skills:** null-hypothesis-testing, data-analysis
308	
309	## S4: Forecasts & agents
310	**Outline:** Forecast returns and volatility with neural networks on the surviving features against simple baselines, then run forecast-driven portfolios and PPO agents in simulated backtests that must beat the stage-2 portfolios out of sample after costs, or stop.
311	**Skills:** null-hypothesis-testing, data-analysis
312	# Quant Research Platform — Probes
313	
314	**Last updated:** 2026-10-17

316	### PB-01: The Sharadar subscription covers every S&P 500 member since 1998, with delisting details, revisions and a timely bulk export
317	**Folder:** `probes/PB-01-sharadar-coverage/` · **Hash:** 32713b98 · **Status:** Draft · **Milestones:** S1 · **Last run:** —
318	- **Question:** Does your subscription return SEP, ACTIONS, TICKERS and SP500 for every stock ever in the index since 1998, delisted ones included; do delistings carry a payout; does TICKERS keep sector history; do `lastupdated` filters return revisions; and is the bulk export fast enough for a 6-hour rebuild?
319	- **Informs:** AD-03, AD-05, AD-08 · DATA-01, DATA-02, DATA-03, DATA-04, DATA-07, UNIV-01, UNIV-03, INGST-02, INGST-04, PANEL-03, VALID-05, QR-03 · `data.StockId`, `data.StockHistory`, `data.ReturnsPanel`, `data.Membership`
320	- **Docs read (2026-10-17):** [Sharadar prices](https://sharadar.com/prices) (tables, delisted coverage from Dec 1997, SP500 from Jan 1998, `close`/`closeadj`/`closeunadj`, update times); [Sharadar fundamentals](https://sharadar.com/fundamentals) (TICKERS `permaticker`, ACTIONS contents); [data-link-python FOR_DEVELOPERS](https://github.com/Nasdaq/data-link-python/blob/main/FOR_DEVELOPERS.md) (`get_table` filters, `qopts.columns`, cursor paging, `export_table`). The tables-API reference refused the fetch (403, JS-rendered).
321	- **Spends:** ~230 API calls against the subscription's rate limits and one full SEP bulk export (several hundred MB to a few GB, kept in `out/`); no money beyond the existing subscription. Licensed rows stay in `out/`; evidence holds counts and value-masked shapes only.
322	- **Checks:** table below
323	- **Verdict:** —
324	- **Caveats:** sample of 100 members (seeded), not the whole universe; SP500 replay treats an `added` row as effective on its date, which C2 measures rather than assumes; no retries or backoff (the adapter needs INGST's 5 retries); split-value convention is inferred in C7, not documented.
325	
326	| Check | What | How | Pass | Runner | Result |
327	|---|---|---|---|---|---|
328	| C1 | All four tables reachable with the columns the design uses | `get_table` per table, 5 rows; compare columns | every table returns rows; no expected column missing (AD-03) | user | — |
329	| C2 | Membership history depth and counts | Full SP500 table; replay adds/removes between quarterly snapshots | ≥ 15 years from first snapshot to latest (DATA-01); no snapshot outside 495–510 (UNIV-01); replay disagreement measured | user | — |
330	| C3 | Permanent identity | TICKERS where table = SEP: duplicate tickers and permatickers; SP500 tickers resolved | 0 duplicates; ≤ 0.5% of SP500 tickers unresolved (UNIV-03) | user | — |
331	| C4 | Bars cover each member's history | SEP for 50 delisted + 50 listed members | ≥ 98 of 100 have bars from 252 sessions before joining (or listing) to their last price date (DATA-02) | user | — |
332	| C5 | Delistings recorded | ACTIONS for the 50 delisted members | ≥ 95% have a delisting-type row (DATA-03) | user | — |
333	| C6 | Delisting payouts | `acquisitionby` rows with a positive `value` | ≥ 80% carry a value, else most delisting returns are estimated −100% (PANEL-03) | user | — |
334	| C7 | Corporate actions and adjusted returns | AAPL/TSLA 2020-08-31 and NVDA 2024-06-10 splits; on every sampled dividend/split day, (unadj close × split + dividend) / previous unadj close vs `closeadj` return | 3 of 3 splits found (DATA-03); ≥ 95% of action days within 0.01 pp (VALID-05) | user | — |
335	| C8 | Sector history in TICKERS | Permatickers with more than one sector; dated classification columns | any dated history exists (DATA-07); fail sends AD-05 to PB-02 | user | — |
336	| C9 | Revisions via `lastupdated` | SEP filtered `lastupdated ≥ today − 10`, timed | rows returned, those dated earlier counted as revisions (INGST-02, INGST-04); ≤ 300 s, inside QR-03's 15 min | user | — |
337	| C10 | Bulk export time | `export_table('SHARADAR/SEP')`, timed | ≤ 2 h, a third of QR-03's 6-hour rebuild | user | — |

339	### PB-02: SEC EDGAR filing headers give a dated SIC history for S&P 500 members since 1998
340	**Folder:** `probes/PB-02-edgar-sic-history/` · **Hash:** d0d5e889 · **Status:** Draft · **Milestones:** S1 · **Last run:** —
341	- **Question:** If Sharadar keeps only current sectors, can every member be linked to its SEC CIK, and does each year's annual-report header record the SIC code in force then, so sectors can be read as of any date?
342	- **Informs:** AD-05 · DATA-07, FCST-03, QR-03 · `data.sectors`
343	- **Docs read (2026-10-17):** [EDGAR APIs](https://www.sec.gov/search-filings/edgar-application-programming-interfaces) (submissions URL `CIK##########.json`, extra files for older filings; silent on per-filing SIC); [Accessing EDGAR data](https://www.sec.gov/os/accessing-edgar-data) (10 requests/s, User-Agent with contact, raw filing path `/Archives/edgar/data/<cik>/<accession>.txt` with the filing header).
344	- **Spends:** ~1,000–1,800 public EDGAR requests at ≤ 8/s (about 5 minutes), sent with your User-Agent; nothing paid.
345	- **Checks:** table below
346	- **Verdict:** —
347	- **Caveats:** reads PB-01's `out/members.json`, so CIKs come from Sharadar's `secfilings` link; one annual report per year (10-K family only, no 20-F); reads only the first 16 KB of each filing; no retries.
348	
349	| Check | What | How | Pass | Runner | Result |
350	|---|---|---|---|---|---|
351	| C1 | Members link to a CIK | CIK parsed from each member's TICKERS `secfilings` link | ≥ 98% of members (DATA-07) | user | — |
352	| C2 | Annual filings span membership | Submissions JSON plus older files for 30 delisted + 30 listed members | ≥ 90% of sampled companies have an annual report in ≥ 90% of their membership years since 1998 | user | — |
353	| C3 | Headers carry an SIC code | `STANDARD INDUSTRIAL CLASSIFICATION` in each annual report's header | ≥ 95% of company-years (DATA-07) | user | — |
354	| C4 | The history is dated, not overwritten | Distinct header SICs per company, against the current SIC | ≥ 3 of 60 companies show a change (DATA-07's reclassification check) | user | — |
355	| C5 | Throughput | Seconds per company, projected to every member | ≤ 60 minutes for the full universe, inside QR-03's 6 hours | user | — |
356	# Quant Research Platform — References
357	
358	**Status:** Approved · **Last updated:** 2026-10-17
359	
360	### RF-01: Market-mode removal by PCA
361	**File:** `references/market_decomposition.py` · **Hash:** b3993e95 · **Kind:** code · **Milestones:** S2
362	- **Purpose:** removes the top-k principal components (the market mode) from a daily returns panel to leave residual returns, and measures how much cross-correlation remains
363	- **Key parts:** `demean_returns(returns, min_obs=60)`, `standardise(returns)`, `pca_factors(returns, k=1)`, `residualize(returns, factors)`, `remove_market(returns, k=1)`, `residual_correlation_check(residuals)`
364	- **In → Out:** T×N simple daily returns (dates × tickers) → dict of residuals (T×N'), loadings and betas (N'×k), factors (T×k), explained variance (k)
365	- **Use for:** the market-factor removal step and its sanity check (mean absolute residual correlation drops sharply versus raw returns); the residual returns later feature tests run against
366	- **Caveats:** fits means, std, eigenvectors and betas on the full sample (look-ahead; needs rolling or expanding windows); silently drops columns under 60 observations and fills remaining gaps (pre-listing, post-delisting) with 0 returns, biasing correlations; k is fixed, with no random-matrix choice of k; no input validation
367	
368	### RF-02: Sequence-forecaster window builder
369	**File:** `references/tft_dataset.py` · **Hash:** fdf31b69 · **Kind:** code · **Milestones:** S4
370	- **Purpose:** turns a long (ticker, date) panel into fixed-length encoder/decoder windows for a sequence forecaster, keeping static, known-ahead and observed inputs separate, then splits by forecast-origin date
371	- **Key parts:** `WindowSpec` (lookback=63, horizon=5, stride=1, column lists), `add_calendar_features(panel)`, `encode_static(panel, cols)`, `normalise_observed(panel, cols, window=252)`, `build_windows(panel, targets, spec)`, `split_by_origin(arrays, train_end, valid_end)`
372	- **In → Out:** long panel plus targets keyed on (ticker, date) from RF-03 → arrays static (S, n_static), known (S, L+H, n_known), observed (S, L, n_obs), target (S, H, n_tgt), origin and ticker (S), split into train/valid/test
373	- **Use for:** the dataset step for neural return/volatility forecasters and their baselines; the known-versus-observed input separation; origin-date splitting
374	- **Caveats:** `split_by_origin` has no purge or embargo, so the last `horizon` train targets reach into validation (worse with stride-1 overlap); `encode_static` builds its vocabulary on the full sample and fails on unseen categories; static values come from each ticker's first row (not point-in-time); `build_windows` silently drops short tickers and rows with no target and never checks windows are gap-free; `normalise_observed` zero-fills warm-up and zero-std rows without a flag; target alignment depends on RF-03's `fwd_ret_1d` timing
375	
376	### RF-03: Forward return and volatility targets
377	**File:** `references/tft_targets.py` · **Hash:** fd947a4a · **Kind:** code · **Milestones:** S3, S4
378	- **Purpose:** builds the forward labels a forecaster is trained to predict: forward log return and forward realised volatility per ticker over several horizons
379	- **Key parts:** `log_returns(prices, price_col)`, `forward_return(prices, horizon, price_col)`, `forward_volatility(prices, horizon, annualise=True, price_col)`, `make_targets(prices, horizons=(1,5,21), price_col)`, `demean_cross_section(targets, cols)`, `drop_incomplete(targets)`
380	- **In → Out:** long daily closes (ticker, date, adj_close) → (ticker, date, `fwd_ret_{h}d`, `fwd_vol_{h}d` per horizon), vol annualised with 252 days
381	- **Use for:** defining forecast targets and their timing; the purge gap a train/test split needs (a label at t is known only after t+h); residual-return labels for feature tests
382	- **Caveats:** horizons count rows, not trading days, so gaps and delistings are treated as consecutive; zero, negative or missing prices pass into the log unchecked; `drop_incomplete` silently drops each ticker's last max(h) days, leaning toward survivorship around delistings; `demean_cross_section` is an equal-weight demean over whoever is present (no point-in-time universe); labels look ahead by design and must never be features
383
